# Rules paragraph diff, 2026-07-16 to 2026-10-09 (#645)

Generated, never hand-edited:

```
node scripts/rules-diff.mjs data/Riftbound-Core-Rules-2026-07-16.txt data/Riftbound-Core-Rules-2026-10-09.txt data/Riftbound-Tournament-Rules-2026-07-16.txt data/Riftbound-Tournament-Rules-2026-10-09.txt docs/rules-diff/2026-07-16-to-2026-10-09.md docs/rules-diff/2026-07-16-to-2026-10-09.json
```

Fates: `renumbered` (same text, new number), `xref` (only a rule number or punctuation inside the text changed), `reworded` (words changed; paired by token Jaccard, `sim` printed), `deleted` (no new paragraph at sim >= 0.5). `number reused` marks an old number that still exists in the new edition holding other text, so an un-updated citation keeps resolving and cites the wrong rule. A citer is a token match of the paragraph number (the tokenizer of `test/rule-refs.test.ts`); `also a <other book> number` marks a number that exists in both books, where the token alone cannot say which book is meant.

- **206** -> **206** (sim 0.98) [also a Tournament Rules number]
  - diff: Effects that need to determine a [-card’s-] {+card or token’s+} cost for any purpose always use its [-printed or copied cost,-] {+printed—or if a copy effect is applied, copied—cost,+} even if that cost is increased, decreased, or ignored as the card is played. Example: Sky Splitter is a spell that costs [8] and says in part “This spell's Energy cost is reduced by the highest Might among units you control.” Lux, Illuminated is a unit that says “When you play a spell that costs 5 or more, give me +3 [M] this turn.” If a player who controls Lux plays Sky Splitter, Lux’s ability will trigger, regardless of how much was actually paid for Sky Splitter. Example: Defy is a spell which reads “Counter a spell that costs no more than [4] and no more than [A].” Rocket Barrage is a spell that costs [4][C] and has a Repeat cost of [4][C]. Rocket Barrage is a legal target for Defy even if Rocket Barrage’s Repeat cost is paid, because Defy only checks the printed or copied cost of its target. Example: Atakhan is a unit that reads in part “You may kill a friendly unit as an additional cost to play me. If you do, I cost [1] less for each Energy it costs and [Y] less for each Power it costs.” If a player plays Atakhan and chooses to kill a Reflection token that is currently a copy of Noxus Hopeful, Atakhan will cost [4] less.
  - citers: none

- **355.11.b** -> **355.11.b** (sim 1)
  - diff: If the group of targets no longer collectively fulfill the targeting restriction as the spell or ability resolves, that spell or ability’s controller [-can-] {+must+} choose a {+legal+} subset of the original targets that fulfills the targeting requirement for the spell or ability to affect. Example: A player plays Fox-Fire, a spell that says in part “Kill any number of units at a battlefield with total Might 4 or less.” That player chooses four 1 [M] Recruit tokens at a single battlefield. As a Reaction, another player gives two of those Recruits +1 [M], so the Recruits’ Mights are 1, 1, 2, and 2. Then Fox-Fire resolves. The Recruits no longer have total Might 4 or less, so Fox-Fire’s controller must choose a legal subset of the original targets to affect. They could choose to kill the two 2 [M] Recruits, or the two 1 [M] Recruits plus one 2 [M] Recruit. The units they choose are Fox-Fire’s remaining legal targets. They can’t choose to affect units at the same battlefield that weren’t initially chosen as targets. They can, however, choose to affect units that were initially chosen as targets that left the chosen battlefield before Fox-Fire resolved as long as those units are all located at the same battlefield.
  - citers: verified combos (3): decree-of-discord-megatusk-evacuate-and-gank, moonlight-affliction-tricksy-tentacles-evacuation, tricksy-tentacles-yuumi-forced-defend-subset; files (6): docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-07-uncited-rules-blocks.md, docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-09-uncited-rules-300-499.md, docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-13-gap-vein-remainder.md, docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-14-uncarried-rules-citation-audit.md, docs/plays/2026-10-07-the-garrison-goes-home-on-turn-six-and-four-bodies-come-back.md, docs/rules-notes/rules-findings.md

- **359.3.e.9.a** -> **359.3.e.9.a** (sim 0.92)
  - diff: If another spell or ability attempts to reference the number of game objects, players, or zones that a Finalized Chain Item targets, it will include any mistargeted [-choices,-] {+choices in the zone specified implicitly or explicitly by the instruction that targeted them,+} but not any targets that have changed to {+or from+} a non-board zone. Example: When a player moves their Volibear, Furious to an occupied enemy battlefield and combat initiates, Volibear’s attack trigger goes on the chain targeting three of the units at that battlefield. In reaction, the defending player plays Flash moving two of the three units back. That player cannot then target the attack trigger with Repulse, which reads “Choose a friendly unit at a battlefield. Counter an enemy spell or ability that chooses it and no other friendly unit.” If the defending player instead played Heedless Resurrection twice, killing the two units, Repulse can legally target the attack trigger, because two of the targets have changed to a non-board zone.

- **359.3.e.12** -> **359.3.e.12** (sim 0.99)
  - diff: If the spell checks information about a target that is no longer legal or a card or permanent whose location, zone, or status has changed such that that information is no longer available, that check returns "null" and all [-calculations-] {+instructions+} based on it are [-ignored.-] {+Ignored.+} Examples: A unit that is no longer on the board is treated as having null Might, null cost, etc. A unit that is no longer on the board has no location, is neither exhausted nor readied, etc. Baited Hook says "[1][C], [E]: Kill a friendly unit. Look at the top 5 cards of your Main Deck. You may banish a unit from among them that has Might up to 1 more than the killed unit and play it, ignoring its cost. Then recycle the rest." While Baited Hook’s ability is on the chain, an opponent reacts with a spell that returns the friendly unit to its owner's hand. Because the friendly unit is no longer a legal target, it can't be killed and its Might is treated as null. Baited Hook’s controller looks at the top 5 cards of their Main Deck, but can’t choose any unit from among them. Strike Down reads “Choose an equipped friendly unit. It deals damage equal to its Might to an enemy unit. Then detach an Equipment from it.” While Strike Down is on the chain targeting a unit with only one Equipment attached to it, that unit has its Equipment detached via Angle Shot. When Strike Down goes to resolve, the targeted unit is no longer legal, so the unit’s Might will return as “null” and the instructions related to it are ignored.

- **465.2.c.5** -> **467.2.c.5** (sim 0.97)
  - diff: When assigning damage in this way, {+passive abilities and+} replacement effects that would apply to the resulting damage are considered to apply to the assignment instead. Example: A unit with 2 [M] is being assigned damage in the combat damage step. The unit has “prevent the first 3 damage I would take each combat.” The unit would need to be assigned 5 damage in order to have lethal damage assigned to it. Example: The attacking player is assigning their 3 [M] worth of damage to two defending units with 2 [M] each. One of the units has a delayed replacement effect applied to it that reads “Double all damage that would be dealt to it this turn.” The attacking player assigns two damage to the other defending unit, then when assigning damage to the unit with the delayed replacement effect they assign 2 damage to it; 1 damage that doubles to 2 damage as it is assigned to the unit. When that damage is dealt, it doesn’t get doubled again—the doubling is considered to have already happened during damage assignment. Example: The attacking player is assigning their 3 [M] worth of damage to a 2 [M] unit. That unit has a prevent value of two being applied to it, as well as the effect of Lotus Trap, doubling the damage dealt to them. Both of these replacement effects apply to the assignment of damage, in the order of the controller of the 2 [M] unit’s choice. If they choose to order the replacement effects so that the prevent value is applied first, the unit will prevent 2 of the assigned damage, then the last 1 point of damage will be doubled to 2. The unit will have 4 damage assigned to it. When damage is dealt, the unit will take 2 damage. If they choose the other order, the unit will have 6 damage assigned to it, 2 of which will be prevented. When damage is dealt, the unit will take 4 damage.
  - citers: verified combos (1): lotus-trap-morgana-excess-damage; files (3): docs/phase0/rules-primer.md, docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-05-hidden-ambush-candidates.md, docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-07-uncited-rules-blocks.md

  - diff: {+When a+} Copy [-effects will copy-] {+effect is applied to a Game Object,+} the [-copyable-] traits {+specified by the Copy Effect replace or are added to the Copyable Traits+} of [-a-] {+the+} Game [-Object. By default, those-] {+Object being Copied as specified by the effect. If no traits+} are [-the printed-] {+specified by a Copy Effect, all+} traits of the Game [-Object. When a Game-] Object [-becomes a copy-] {+are replaced with those+} of [-something, its copyable traits are updated to-] the [-new traits it has received.-] {+Copied Game Object.+} Example: A player triggers Leblanc, Deceiver’s hold effect and plays a Reflection token, making it a copy of Honest Broker. That player then plays Mirror Image, targeting the Reflection token. When the Mirror Image Reflection token is played, it copies all of the copyable traits of the original Reflection token - which are currently those of Honest Broker which it is a copy of. That player will have three units named Honest Broker in play, two of which are token Copies with Temporary.

  - diff: If a [-hidden spell or a-] play effect of a hidden permanent chooses any targets, those targets must be chosen from among options at [-that battlefield,-] {+the battlefield it was played to,+} unless the ability explicitly restricts targeting in a way that makes this impossible. Move me to its location and it to my original location.” Because its play effect has a targeting restriction that can never be fulfilled by a unit at its battlefield, its target may be chosen freely from among the available options. {+Example: A spell reads in part “The next unit you play this turn can be played to any battlefield.” After playing that spell, a player plays Blastcone Fae from facedown to a battlefield other than the one where it was hidden. - **811.1.d.2.a** -> **811.1.d.4** (sim 0.96)
  - diff: Each target is treated separately and individually when processing this rule. Example: Smoke and Mirrors is a spell that reads in part “Choose a unit you control and another unit you control at a different location.” If Smoke and Mirrors is played from [-hidden,-] {+facedown,+} the first unit chosen can be chosen at the battlefield Smoke and Mirrors was played from, so it must be. The second unit chosen explicitly restricts targeting in a way that makes this impossible, so it can be chosen from any location.

- **811.2** -> **811.2** (sim 0.98)
  - diff: Abilities and instructions of hidden cards other than the choices listed above function as normal. Example: Stand United is a spell that has Hidden and says “Buff a friendly unit. Buffs give an additional +1 might to friendly units this turn.” If it’s played from [-Hidden,-] {+facedown,+} the first part of its ability must choose a friendly unit at the same battlefield, but the second part of its ability affects all friendly units with buffs, no matter where they are.

- **427.3.a** -> **428.3.a** (sim 0.98)
  - diff: Separate instances of an object or effect banishing cards do not reference other banished cards by objects of the same name or effects originating from cards of the same name. Example: A spell reads "Banish the top card of your Main Deck. When you conquer this turn, draw it." This card effect allows you to draw the banished card from banishment if a condition is met. If more than one of these effects is played, each one triggers separately. Each card will be drawn separately. If the turn player passes the turn without playing the card, the card remains in banishment indefinitely. If the same player plays another copy of the same spell, banishes another card, and satisfies its condition, they could play the new banished card, but not the original banished card. See rule [-393.-] {+394.+} Linked Abilities for more information.
  - citers: files (3): docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-09-uncited-rules-300-499.md, docs/phase0/walks/2026-09-14-uncarried-rules-citation-audit.md, docs/rules-notes/rules-pairs.md
- **435.1** -> **436.1** (sim 1) [number reused]
  - diff: Detaching is the act of unlinking two cards that are currently linked through the act of Attaching. This causes one to cease being Attached, and potentially causes the other to cease being a [-Top-Most-] {+Top- Most+} card.

### Core Rules: new-only paragraphs (136)

- **103.1** Cards in a player’s deck or rules of a Game Mode may indicate additional choices to be made while building the deck. Example: A unit reads in part “If [Name] is in your deck, choose 3 different legends in addition to your starting legend.” This decision is made while building the deck. Example: The rules of a game mode read in part “Choose an additional Chosen Champion with a different Champion Tag and Domain than your Champion Legend. Place it in the Champion Zone at the start of the game alongside your other Chosen Champion. Your deck’s Domain Identity includes that additional Chosen Champion’s Domain, if applicable.” The Chosen Champion chosen this way is decided while building the deck.
- **103.2.b.5** If a card has no Domain, then that card is permitted in decks of any Domain Identity.
- **108.4.d** The number of cards in a player’s Main Deck is Public Information.
- **133.3.a** When characteristics are referred to in this way, it refers to any Game Object with the appropriate characteristic on the board, unless the zone is specified implicitly or explicitly by the effect doing the referring or the nature of the characteristic. Example: A "Yeti" refers to a game object with the Yeti tag on the board. Example: A "spell" refers to a spell on the chain, because spells cannot exist as game objects on the board. Example: A "spell in your trash" refers to a spell card in your trash because the effect specifies the zone in question.
- **135.2.b.5.b** If a conditional clause appears in instructions prior to one or more clauses with multiple instructions, that condition applies to all instructions in subsequent clauses in the same sentence. Example: A spell reads in part “If you’ve gained a point this turn, draw 1, then discard 1.” In order for either of the instructions that follow the “If you’ve gained a point this turn,” conditional clause to execute, the condition must be true. Example: Rogue Assassin is a legend that reads in part “[Action][>] [E]: If it's your turn, move a friendly unit in a showdown to base and if I'm [Empowered], ready it.” The first conditional clause in the activated ability applies to all subsequent instructions in the same sentence. The second conditional clause, “if I’m [Empowered],” only applies to the instruction that immediately follows it.
- **135.2.b.7** Instructions of a spell or ability may reference actions taken during Finalization. They will do so by checking whether “you” performed those game actions. These instructions check whether the game action was performed, not by whom. If another player gains control of the spell or ability after the game action was performed during finalization, it will still be considered true for the purposes of these instructions. Example: Rampage is a spell that reads “As you play this, you may pay [C] as an additional cost.” and “Choose a friendly unit and an enemy unit. If you paid the additional cost, give the friendly unit +2 [Might] this turn. They deal damage equal to their Mights to each other.” If another player gains control of Rampage, although they are not the one who paid the cost, the “if you paid the additional cost,” condition will still be fulfilled. Example: Back Off is a spell that reads in part “If you played this from your hand, draw 1.” On resolution, Back Off checks if it was played from hand. If another player gained control of Back Off, as long as the card was played from hand, the condition will be true and the controller of the spell will draw 1.
- **143.2.a** A Unit’s Might can be referenced by cards and effects in one of three forms: A Unit has a Printed Might, that is the value in the top right corner of the unit card/token; a Base Might, that is a trait that is alterable by effects in the Trait-Altering layer; and an Effective Might, that is the value calculated in the arithmetic layer including all Might altering Game Effects. See rule 475. Layers for more information.
- **143.2.a.1** Card text may refer to the latter two of these three forms. When cards or these rules refer to a Unit’s Effective Might, they will do so by simply referring to that unit’s “Might.” Example: A card reads in part “Choose a unit. Its base Might becomes 5 this turn.” This applies an effect in the trait-altering layer. Example: A card reads in part “Choose a unit. Increase its Might to 5 this turn.” This applies an effect in the arithmetic layer.
- **143.5** Units cannot be Attached to other permanents. See rule 435. Attach for more information on the attach game action. See rule 716. Attachment for more information on attachment.
- **143.5.a** If a Unit would be Attached to a permanent, nothing happens.
- **143.5.b** If a non-Unit Game Object that is Attached becomes a Unit through any means, it detaches as it becomes a Unit.
- **174.2.b** Legends are established at the start of a game, and remain in place for the duration of regular play. Legends may be Created during the course of play. They will be Created ready unless the effect specifies otherwise.
- **187.12** A Bomb gear token is a domainless gear token with “[Deploy]” and “[Deathknell][>] Deal 2 to an enemy unit here and kill all other Bombs you control here.” See rule 830. Deploy for more information.
- **190.3.a.2** A Unit changing controller while located at a Battlefield will also apply Contested status to that Battlefield if it isn’t Contested and if the new controller doesn’t control that Battlefield.
- **191.1** Players control their own Champion Legend.
- **191.5.a.2** If an Ability allows players other than the Controller or Owner of its Source to play it, the player that plays the Ability Controls the Ability.
- **204.1.b** On activated abilities, the Base Cost is the resource or instruction written before the “:”. If there are multiple choices for costs to pay for an activated ability before the “:”, the Base Cost is whatever cost the player chooses from among those options. Example: Legion Marauder is a unit that reads in part “[Empower] — [1] or [C].” This is functionally short for “[1] or [C]: Empower me. Play only if not empowered.” When finalizing the ability to the chain, the controller of the chain item will choose one of the base costs to pay. That is the base cost of the ability.
- **323.6** 5. Recall all of the following Game Objects, unless an effect would indicate otherwise: Unattached non-Unit Gear located at Battlefields Non-Unit Runes located at Battlefields Unattached Permanents and Runes in Bases other than their controller’s
- **355.10.g** It is a legend “you” control. e.g. “Ready your legend.” doesn’t target any legend. If a player controls multiple legends, they choose one as the spell or ability resolves. e.g. “Ready a friendly legend.” targets the friendly legend. e.g. “Exhaust an enemy legend.” targets the enemy legend.
- **355.11.b.1** Any legal subset must include the maximum number of legal targets possible for that subset. Example: A player plays Bellow’s Breath, a spell that reads in part “Deal 1 to up to 3 units at the same location.” They choose 3 units at the same battlefield. In reaction to Bellow’s Breath, the controller of those units plays Flash, moving two of them to base. On resolution of Bellow’s Breath, Bellow’s Breath’s controller cannot choose one unit in base and not the other. They may choose either of the legal subsets; the one unit remaining at the battlefield, or the two units in base.
- **355.17** If a Game Effect has introduced a mandatory requirement on any of these choices, that mandatory requirement must be followed if possible. A mandatory requirement is one that specifies what choice can be made or how that choice is made, but doesn't specifically disallow other game states. Example: Here to Help is a spell that reads in part “You may play a unit from your hand to a battlefield you control.” If its controller decides to play a unit, they must play it to a battlefield they control. Example: Rek’Sai, Swarm Queen is a unit that reads “When I attack, you may reveal the top 2 cards of your Main Deck. You may banish one, then play it. If it is a unit, you may play it here. Recycle the rest.” The “you may play it here,” portion of this ability is not a mandatory requirement, because the player isn’t required to play the unit to that location.
- **355.17.a** If there are multiple mandatory requirements that do not conflict, players must make a choice that will satisfy those mandatory requirements. Any individual group of such complementary mandatory requirements that contains as many non-contradictory mandatory requirements as possible forms a set. If there are two or more sets of mandatory requirements that are mutually contradictory, choose one of those sets and ignore the rest. Example: Here to Help is played from facedown at Targon’s Peak. Its controller chooses to play a Dune Drake from hand. The other battlefield is Dragon Roost. When finalizing Dune Drake, they choose to pay the [A][A] additional cost from Dragon Roost. There are two competing mandatory requirements here: playing Dune Drake to Targon’s Peak, or playing it to Dragon Roost. Dune Drake’s controller must choose one of those requirements to satisfy, and ignore the rest. Example: If Here to Help were played from hand, and its controller controlled both battlefields, if they paid the [A][A] additional cost, they would have no choice but to play Dune Drake to Dragon Roost as it would satisfy both mandatory requirements.
- **355.17.b** If there are one or more restrictions on choices being made during this process, those restrictions must be followed. Failure to do so means the card will fail to finalize. Example: Perched Grimwyrm is a unit that reads “Play me only to a battlefield you conquered this turn.” If a player plays Perched Grimwyrm via Here to Help and they haven’t conquered a battlefield this turn, Perched Grimwyrm will fail to finalize. Although Here to Help presents a mandatory requirement, Perched Grimwyrm’s restriction on being played overrides it. Example: Mageseeker Warden is a unit that reads in part “While I'm at a battlefield, opponents can only play units to their base.” If a player attempts to play a Perched Grimwyrm to a battlefield they’ve conquered this turn while their opponent controls a Mageseeker Warden at a battlefield, the Perched Grimwyrm will fail to finalize.
- **356.4.c.2** Discounts that apply to a given component of a spell’s cost cannot reduce that component to less than 0.
- **356.4.g** If a discount applies a variable cost reduction to the card, that value is checked now. Example: Rhasa, the Sunderer reads “I cost [1] less for each card in your trash.” The value of this discount depends on the number of cards in the trash. That value is checked during this step when the discount is applied to Rhasa’s cost. If the number of cards in his controller’s trash changes at a later point in the finalization process, that will not affect the value checked during this step or the total cost calculated in this step.
- **358.1** Check that the card made appropriate choices. Example: If the card chose targets, ensure that the targets are legal. Example: If the card made choices that had restrictions or requirements, ensure that restrictions were followed and that the requirements were met as appropriate. See rule 355. Make Relevant Choices for more information.
- **358.3.b** This check only extends to the resolution of the current card. Any Reflexive Triggers, cards played, or abilities triggered as a result of the execution must be evaluated on their own merits as they finalize.
- **359.3.e.2.a** This includes targeting restrictions that are applied to the spell by game effects other than the spell’s instructions themselves. Example: Temporal Breach is a spell that reads in part “Banish a unit, then its owner plays it to the same location, ignoring its cost.” By default, the only targeting restrictions on the spell are that the target is a unit, and that the unit is able to be chosen by the spell. However, if Temporal Breach is played from facedown, it gains the restriction that it must choose from among game objects at the battlefield it was played from. If the unit it chooses moves to a different location, that unit will be an illegal target on resolution of Temporal Breach.
- **370.1.c.1** If the Replacement Effect of a Game Object applies to an event that involves that same Game Object while it is in a secret zone, it applies to and replaces such an event simultaneously with that event occurring instead. The replacing event still occurs in the order specified by the instruction that generated it, and the original event does not occur if it is replaced. Example: Nocturne, Horrifying reads in part “As you look at or reveal me from the top of your deck, you may banish me. If you do, you may play me for [A].” Because Nocturne’s replacement effect replaces an event that applies while he is in a secret zone, it applies to the event of him being looked at or revealed from the top of a player’s Main Deck simultaneously with that event occurring. Example: A card reads in part “If you would burn this, you may burn this and an additional card instead.” Because the replacement effect applies to the card while it is in a secret zone, it applies simultaneously with the event occurring. Another card reads “If a player would put a card into their trash from their Main Deck, banish it instead.” This replacement effect is active on the board, and thus applies before the qualifying event occurs. Because these replacement effects are applied at different timings, they are applied in order. The card will be banished first, and subsequently its replacement effect will have nothing to replace.
- **378.1** If the source of the Activated Ability is uncontrolled, and it isn’t a Battlefield, the owner of the source decides when and whether to activate the ability.
- **378.2** Some activated abilities specify that one or more players other than the controller or owner of their source can activate them. Those players decide when and whether to activate the ability.
- **381.1** If the source of the Activated Ability is controlled by a player, that is the controller’s Main Phase during an Open State.
- **381.2** If the source of the Activated Ability is uncontrolled and it isn’t a Battlefield, that is the owner’s Main Phase during an Open State.
- **381.3** If the Activated Ability specifies that one or more players other than the controller or owner of the source can activate it, that is during the activating player's Main Phase during an Open State.
- **383.2.c.3** If a Game Object has a Triggered Ability whose condition is the Game Object leaving the board, such a Triggered Ability is processed immediately before the Game Object changes zones. Example: If a game object has a deathknell ability, which triggers on the game object dying, it will be processed and placed on the chain just prior to the game object being placed in the trash, during the event of the game object dying. Example: Treasure Trove has a triggered ability that triggers as it “leaves the board.” Such a triggered ability is processed and placed on the chain just before Treasure Trove completes the action of leaving the board by being moved to another zone.
- **383.2.c.3.a** Triggered Abilities that trigger from Game Objects leaving the board trigger and are placed on the chain after any Replacement Effects are processed. If a Replacement Effect causes the Game Object to not leave the board in the appropriate way, the relevant Triggered Abilities will not trigger. Example: If a unit’s death is replaced by an event of that unit being healed, exhausted, and recalled, that unit’s Deathknell abilities will not trigger, as the unit’s death event is replaced and does not occur. Example: If Treasure Trove’s being killed is replaced with it being banished, it will still trigger just before the banish action is executed.
- **383.2.c.3.b** When the Triggered Ability is being processed, note any information about the source of the Triggered Ability or the state of the game as needed in order to process the resulting Triggered Ability. Example: A unit reads in part “When I leave the board, deal 2 to a unit where I was.” Its controller plays Retreat on the unit, returning it to hand. Before the unit is returned to hand, the triggered ability is placed on the chain and the necessary information about that unit is noted. When the triggered ability is being finalized, we can identify the former location of the unit in question with the noted information.
- **383.4.g.2** The named part of the condition is fulfilled, but no actual such event occurs. Example: Red Brambleback is a unit that reads in part “Your conquer effects for conquering here trigger an additional time.” Reckoner’s Arena is a battlefield that reads “When you hold here, activate the conquer effects of units here.” The conquer effects that were activated in this way were not the result of a conquer occurring, so they will not trigger an additional time from Red Brambleback’s passive ability if it is located there.
- **386** Some Replacement Effects, Passive Abilities, and Triggered Abilities will begin with “once each turn,” “N times each turn,” “once on each of your turns,” or “N times on each of your turns.”
- **386.1** Such Replacement Effects and Triggered Abilities will only be applied to the specified number of events each turn, or performed the specified number of times each turn. Once they have been performed that many times or been applied to that many events, they cannot trigger or be applied to a later event in the same turn.
- **386.2** If such a Triggered Ability says “you may,” or “they may,” as the first part of its effect, or such a Replacement Effect says a player “may” replace the qualifying event, the controller of the ability has the choice of whether or not to perform or apply it.
- **386.2.a** For Replacement Effects, when an event the Replacement Effect could apply to occurs, the player who controls the Replacement Effect may choose not to apply it to the event. For Triggered Abilities, during Finalization of the Triggered Ability, the player who controls the Triggered Ability may choose not to perform it.
- **386.2.b** If they do not apply the Replacement Effect, it has not been applied this turn. If they do not perform the Triggered Ability, it is removed from the chain. Example: Zilean, Time mage reads “Once each turn, if you would play a token unit while I'm at a battlefield, you may play that token and an additional copy of it instead.” When his controller plays a token, they can choose not to apply the replacement effect to that event. If they do, they can choose to apply it to a later event of a token being played. Example: A player controls a unit that reads in part “Once each turn, when an enemy unit dies, you may banish it.” When an enemy Recruit token dies, the triggered ability goes on the chain. If they choose not to perform the ability on finalization, it is removed from the chain. When a Stalwart Poro dies later in the turn, they can choose to perform it then.
- **386.3** For such Passive Abilities, they will be active until they have been applied to the specified number of events, after which point they will become inactive.
- **386.3.a** If such a Passive Ability says “you may” or “they may” as the first part of the ability after the “once each turn,” the controller of the source of the Passive Ability chooses whether or not to apply the ability to the event in question. If they do not, the Passive Ability is Ignored during that event. See rule 764. Ignoring Effects for more information.
- **388.4** If a Reflexive Trigger says “you may” or “they may” as the first part of its Effect, the controller of its source will choose whether or not to perform the Reflexive Trigger during finalization. Example: Death From Below is a spell that reads in part “Then, if it had 3 might or less, do this: You may play this from your trash for [A].” The reflexive trigger is added to the chain when the spell is resolving, and then on finalization of the reflexive trigger its controller will decide if they want to perform the effect. If they don’t, it is removed from the chain.
- **413** Types of Actions (A Non-Exhaustive List)
- **418.8** Some Deal actions may be written as “Deal X … for each …” Such actions Deal one instance of Damage. Example: Riven, Shattered reads in part “When I attack, choose an enemy unit here. Deal 2 to it for each Equipment attached to me.” If Riven has three Equipment attached to her, she will deal one instance of 6 damage, not three instances of 2 damage.
- **420.3.b.1** Such game effects may specify choices or effects such as alternate costs, ignored costs, play locations, targets, or other choices or effects normally made or processed during Finalization.
- **420.3.b.2** Such game effects may specify those choices or effects based on the condition of the board. If they do so, that information is checked on finalization of the resulting Game Object, unless it is specified as being checked earlier. Example: Bone Skewer is a spell that reads in part “Choose a battlefield. An opponent reveals their hand. You may choose a unit from it. They play that unit to that battlefield, ignoring any and all costs.” The chosen battlefield isn’t checked on finalization of the resulting game object but when the spell itself is resolving. Example: A spell reads in part “Play a unit from your trash to an enemy occupied battlefield, ignoring its Energy cost.” On finalization, they can choose any enemy occupied battlefield to play the unit to. If no such battlefield exists as the unit is being finalized, it will fail to finalize. Example: Rek’Sai, Swarm Queen is a unit that reads “When I attack, you may reveal the top 2 cards of your Main Deck. You may banish one, then play it. If it is a unit, you may play it here. Recycle the rest.” The attack trigger adds a permission to the normal valid locations for the played unit, that being “here.” The “here” in Rek’Sai’s ability is a referent, whose information is checked when the ability resolves. As such, it will not check on finalization of the unit being played.
- **420.5** If an effect refers to a card being played and doesn’t do so by means of checking whether cards were played, it refers to the card being added to the chain and queued for Finalization, that is, the Play Game Action. Example: Astral Heron is a unit that reads “When you play your first card each turn, if I'm at a battlefield, the next card you play this turn costs [2][A][A] less.” This effect applies a discount to the next card the play game action, discretionary or limited, is performed on.
- **430.2.a** Priority and Focus will not pass from Add abilities being finalized or resolving. Any ongoing Task or step in the FEPR process is paused while they are being finalized and while they are resolving.
- **430.2.b** Spells that Add resources will be finalized and will resolve at the normal timing for spells.
- **433.1.b** Some Game Effects may also cause Damage to be Doubled; in this case, those effects are the equivalent to a replacement effect that reads “If I would take damage from a source, I take double that damage from that source instead.” or “If [I/this] would deal damage, [I/this] deal double that damage instead.” The source of the Damage in these cases remains the same after Doubling.
- **434.1.d** If the attributes being swapped are Units’ Mights, this effect is the equivalent to giving those units a Might increasing effect and a Might decreasing effect respectively.
- **435.1.h** Units cannot be Attached to other cards. See rule 140. Units for more information.
- **435.1.h.1** If a Unit would be Attached, nothing happens.
- **435.1.h.2** If a non-Unit Game Object that is attached becomes a Unit through any means, it detaches as it becomes a Unit.
- **440.3.b** If the effect specifies a Game Object is Created for a specific duration, it exists while that duration is ongoing, and ceases to exist once that duration ends.
- **440.3.c** If a Non-Token Game Object is Created from a finite set of choices made by a Linked Ability during deck building, only one of those Game Objects may exist in play at a time. If a player would Create a Game Object that already exists on the board or on the chain, that action is Negated.
- **442.1.c.1** Some effects may grant a Game Object permission to be Empowered multiple times. Such an effect ignores this restriction. For these Game Objects, Empowered is not a binary state but a stacking status.
- **443.1.b** Disempowering a Game Object that has multiple Empowered statuses will remove one of those statuses.
- **445.1.b** In order to qualify as a resource to be removed from the Rune Pool, the resource must be [1] or greater, or [C] or [A] or greater. Example: [0] is not a resource that can be removed from the Rune Pool. As a cost, it is paid without performing the pay game action. This cannot be declined.
- **446** Copy
- **446.1** Copying a Game Object is the act of adding or replacing some or all of the copyable traits of another Game Object with those of the Copied Game Object.
- **446.2** Copy Effects are rendered in the Copy Effects layer. See rule 475. Layers for more information.
- **446.3** Some Game Objects may be created as a Copy of another Game Object. This is not a Copy Effect. The Copy in such a case is rendered with the same copyable traits as those of the Game Object it is a Copy of. It has no printed traits of its own that are subsequently replaced. Example: Zilean, Time Mage is a unit that reads “Once each turn, if you would play a token unit while I'm at a battlefield, you may play that token and an additional copy of it instead.” The additional copy played by Zilean’s replacement effect has no printed traits of its own. Its printed traits are those of the token it is a copy of.
- **446.4** Copy is a Limited Action.
- **446.4.a** Players may only Copy Game Objects when directed to by Game Effects.
- **470.1** Scoring is a distinct process from Gaining Points. Scoring may result in a player Gaining Points, but the two are not intrinsically linked.
- **470.2** Card text will refer to the process of Gaining Points in the following way: “Score X points.” The “score” in this text does not refer to the process of Scoring.
- **470.3** Card text will refer to the process of Scoring using the word “score” without referring to some number of points. This does refer to the process of Scoring.
- **478.1** Layers are applied and evaluated continuously. All Game Objects have their layers updated in the same instant, but each Game Object is treated separately and individually when evaluating layers.
- **478.1.a** If evaluating a Game Effect’s applicability depends on information from one or more other Game Objects, that Game Effect has an object level Dependency. Pause evaluating the layers of the dependent Game Object and evaluate the layer effects of those Game Objects whose information is referenced. Continue once the applicability of that Game Effect has been established or disqualified. Example: A 4 [M] unit reads in part “While I’m [Mighty], I’m a Yeti.” Another unit reads in part “I have all tags of units you control.” When the 4 [M] unit is buffed, the layers for all game objects are evaluated. While evaluating the layers for the second unit, it requires us to pause and evaluate the layers for all other units. When we’re evaluating the layers for the 4 [M] unit, after all is said and done, the unit has the Yeti tag. Once we’ve evaluated all units, we go back to evaluating the second unit, and at that time it has the Yeti tag as well.
- **478.1.b** If a Game Object has an object level dependency with a Game Object that itself has an object level dependency with a third Game Object and so on, determine the final point of the dependency chain and evaluate the layer effects of that Game Object first, continuing up the dependency chain until all Game Objects have had their layer effects evaluated.
- **478.1.c** If a dependency chain loops back around on itself, identify the minimum set of Game Objects in the loop and evaluate their layer effects simultaneously, layer by layer. If any of them have Game Effects that reference Game Objects from outside the set, pause and evaluate those Game Objects first before continuing with the set. Example: A 4 [M] unit reads “If there is a [Mighty] enemy unit here, I have +5 [M].” Both players control one of these units at a battlefield during combat. Each one is buffed. A third player plays Siphon Power, which reads “Choose a battlefield. Give friendly units there +1 [M] this turn, and enemy units there -1 [M] this turn, to a minimum of 1 [M].” Both of the units there are given -1 [M]. Because they have effects that reference each other, they have a dependency chain. After evaluating their layers simultaneously, layer by layer, both units will be 4 [M].
- **479.1** 1. Copy Effects
- **479.1.a** This layer deals with all copy effects.
- **479.1.b.1** The copyable traits of a Game Object are all of the printed—or if a copy effect is applied, copied—traits of that Game Object. In the case of a Token, they are the traits the token had when it was Created. Example: The copyable traits include the printed versions of the following traits: Name, Supertype, Type, Tags, Cost, Domain, Rules Text, Effect Text, Might, Might Bonus, Flavor Text, Art
- **479.1.c** Copyable traits of a Game Object are distinct from the actual traits of that Game Object, which may be modified by subsequent applied Layer effects.
- **479.1.d** Effects for this layer can be identified by the phrase "copy" in the text. Example: Svellsongur is an Equipment that reads in part “As this is attached to a unit, copy that unit's text to this Equipment's effect text for as long as this is attached to it.” This is a copy effect that is applied in this layer.
- **479.3** 3. Ability-Altering Effects
- **479.4** 4. Arithmetic Effects
- **479.4.f** Effects for this layer can be identified by the phrase "give,” “swap,” "increase," "decrease," "cost … less," "cost … more," "-X,” or “+X,” in the text. Example: An effect says “I cost [1] less for each card you've played this turn, to a minimum of [1].” The cost is modified in this layer.
- **491.8.b** A Battlefield can only be Scored, via Conquering or Holding, once per team per turn.
- **718.6** Only non-Unit Game Objects may be Attached.
- **718.6.a** If a non-Unit Game Object that is Attached becomes a Unit through any means, it detaches as it becomes a Unit.
- **727.1.c.1.b** If the condition for the Dependent Keyword causes the Triggered Ability to become Inactive after it has been added to the chain as a Pending Item, that chain item will not be affected and will proceed with being played as normal.
- **742.1** Unlike other Game Objects, Counters are not targeted by spells and abilities. If an effect instructs a player to interact with a Counter, the Counter is chosen when the corresponding instruction executes.
- **753** When making new choices in this way, a player must choose to remake all choices made for a chain item, even if they choose to make the same choice that was previously made.
- **753.3** Restrictions and requirements applied to a chain item will also apply to new choices made for that chain item in this way. Example: If a card is played from facedown, it gains the restriction that it can only target game objects at the battlefield it was played from unless its targeting is restricted in such a way that choosing a unit at that battlefield is impossible. If a player makes new choices for such a card, they must still choose from among objects at that battlefield.
- **805.1** Accelerate is an Optional Additional Cost keyword.
- **805.1.a** Accelerate is present on Units.
- **808.1.c.2** [Effect] may be written in the past tense. In doing so, it refers to the moment the Deathknell trigger was added to the chain, not any previous moment.
- **811.1.d.2** If a hidden spell chooses any targets, those targets must be chosen from among options at the battlefield it was played from, unless it explicitly restricts targeting in such a way that makes this impossible. Example: Emperor’s Divide is a spell with hidden that reads in part “Move any number of friendly units at a battlefield to their base.” When this spell is played from facedown, all units chosen for the spell must be located at the same battlefield it was played from.
- **811.1.d.3.a** A play effect of a permanent played from facedown references information from its source when determining what battlefield it was played from and what targets are legal. Example: A unit reads in part “When you play me, kill another friendly unit.” If it is played from the facedown zone, the play effect must choose a friendly unit at the battlefield the unit was played from. If the unit is no longer on the board when the play effect attempts to finalize, it will not be able to choose a legal target. If the unit is no longer on the board when the play effect attempts to resolve, the play effect will not be able to reference the information of where the unit was played from, and it will mistarget.
- **811.5.a** This is true as long as the card is not facedown. A facedown card is not a card with Hidden.
- **821.1.c.2.a** The Equip ability is not actually activated when its cost is determined in this way. An effect that grants an activated ability a cost reduction when it is played will not apply to the Equip cost calculated during the resolution of a Weaponmaster trigger, because the Equip ability is not actually played.
- **821.1.c.2.b** Effects that reduce the cost of Equip abilities without referring to those abilities being played, or the Equip ability being referenced in specific, will be included when calculating costs in this way.
- **827.1.c.5** Game Objects with Empower abilities may have or gain permissions to be Empowered multiple times. Such Game Objects ignore the normal “Play only if not Empowered.” restriction on Empower abilities.
- **830** Deploy
- **830.1** Deploy is a Passive Ability and a Triggered Ability keyword.
- **830.1.a** Deploy is present on Gear.
- **830.1.b** Deploy is short for “This may be played or created to any battlefield,” “Play or create this only to a battlefield,” “If this is not a unit, it is not recalled in cleanups,” and “When an opponent holds here, kill this.”
- **830.1.b.1** Deploy grants permission for Gear to be played to any Battlefield, including enemy occupied and open battlefields.
- **830.1.b.2** Gear with Deploy can be Moved by appropriate effects.
- **830.1.b.3** Non-Unit Gear with Deploy are not recalled from Battlefields in step 5 of a Cleanup. See rule 318. Cleanups for more information.
- **830.2** Multiple instances of Deploy are redundant.
- **830.3** Deploy, and whether or not a card has Deploy, is a characteristic of the card and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.
- **831** Disarm
- **831.1** Disarm is a Triggered Ability keyword.
- **831.1.a** Disarm is present on Units.
- **831.1.b** Disarm is an Attack Trigger.
- **831.1.c** Disarm is formatted as “Disarm X.”
- **831.1.c.1** The X is referenced in the functional text of the ability.
- **831.1.c.2** The X is referred to as the Disarm Value.
- **831.1.c.3** If X is omitted, it is presumed to be 1.
- **831.1.d** Disarm is functionally short for “When I attack, give an enemy unit here -X [M] this turn.”
- **831.1.d.1** Regardless of how many instances of the Disarm keyword a unit has, it will only trigger once using the total Disarm Value.
- **831.2** If a Unit has Disarm, or has been granted Disarm, and is granted Disarm by an additional source, the Disarm Value of all granted Disarm keywords is summed. Example: Clanging Sentry reads “Your Mechs have [Disarm].” If you play a second Clanging Sentry, both of them will have Disarm 2.
- **831.3** Disarm, and whether or not a unit has Disarm, is a characteristic of the Unit and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.
- **832** Show Off
- **832.1** Show Off is a Passive Ability keyword.
- **832.1.a** Show Off is normally present on Cards.
- **832.1.b** Show Off is formatted as “[Show Off] [Object].”
- **832.1.b.1** The “[Object]” in this ability could be one object or multiple; object/s of a given type; or it could have further specifications on qualities of the object/s. The object in question could be Game Objects on the board or cards in hand. Examples: A card reads “[Show Off] a unit.” A card reads “[Show Off] a card with Energy cost [7] or more.” A card reads “[Show Off] five units with different names.”
- **832.1.c** Show Off is functionally short for “As you play [me/this], you may reveal [Object] from your hand and/or pick [Object] from among friendly game objects on the board.”
- **832.1.c.1** Showing Off one or more Cards or Game Objects in this way can be referenced by linked abilities and instructions. Example: A unit reads in part “[Show Off] five units with different names,” and “When you play me, kill all other units. If you showed off, the next unit you play this turn costs [1] less for each killed unit.”
- **832.1.c.2** The decision of whether or not to Show Off is made by the controller of the chain item with Show Off during the process of finalizing it. See rule 355. Make Relevant Choices for more information.
- **832.1.c.3** If [Object] is a set of Cards or Game Objects, that set can be composed of Game Objects on the board as well as Cards in hand, as long as the composed set satisfies the Show Off condition. Example: A unit reads in part “[Show Off] five units with different names.” When showing off as part of playing that unit, units may be revealed from hand or picked from among friendly units on the board, as long as the group of units picked and revealed satisfies the specified quality.
- **832.1.c.4** If some or all of the [Object] set leave the appropriate zones during the finalization process after being shown off, it will not prevent the source of the Show Off ability from becoming a Finalized Chain Item. Example: A spell reads “[Show Off] a Mech” and “As an additional cost to play this, discard 1.” When finalizing the spell, the same Mech being shown off can be discarded to the mandatory additional cost without preventing the spell from being finalized.
- **832.1.d** Linked abilities and instructions can reference information about the shown off object/s. Example: Primordial Roar is a spell that reads “[Show Off] a unit,” and “If you showed off a unit, deal damage equal to that unit's Might to a unit.”
- **832.1.d.1** The information referenced in this way is checked when the card/s in question are shown off during finalization. Any later changes to the shown off objects will not be considered. Example: Primordial Roar is played showing off a friendly unit with 4 [M] and an enemy unit with 3 [M]. In reaction to Primordial Roar, an opponent plays Frigid Touch, giving the friendly unit -2 [M] this turn. Because the information was checked when the friendly unit was shown off, the spell still deals 4 damage to the enemy unit, killing it.
- **832.2** Multiple instances of Show Off are treated separately.
- **832.2.a** If a linked effect or instruction refers to an instance of Show Off, it refers to the keyword granted by the same source unless specified otherwise or there is no keyword granted by the same source. If the linked effect or instruction specifies information about the shown off card, it is linked to only that instance of the Show Off keyword. If there is no linked keyword, an ability that references showing off is linked to all Show Off keywords on the appropriate card. Example: A card reads in part “Spells you play have ‘[Show Off] a Mighty unit.’ and ‘If you showed off, this costs [A] less.” If a spell that reads in part “[Show Off] a spell with Energy cost [7] or more,” and “If you showed off, this can’t be countered.” is played by the same controller, showing off a Mighty unit will not be sufficient to satisfy both abilities that reference showing off because they refer to instances of the show off keyword granted by different sources.
- **832.3** Show Off, and whether or not a card has Show Off, is a characteristic of the card and may be checked or referenced by other Game Effects.

### Tournament Rules: new-only paragraphs (54)

- **204.5.c** Intervening in games when they see an error occur to apply the appropriate fixes and penalties. This does not apply to Missed Trigger errors. See 702.2. For more information on Missed Trigger errors.
- **204.5.c.1** Judges should intervene AFTER an error occurs and should not attempt to prevent a player from erroring. This does not apply to proactively intervening in cases of Unsporting Conduct.
- **401.1.b** Even when a low OPL constructed event does not require deck registration, players are expected to use the same deck throughout the event unless given permission by the tournament organizer.
- **401.2.a** They must also include registered records of any cards selected for special additional deckbuilding rules. (Example: The three Legends selected for Neeko, Blending In must be registered on a player’s decklist and can’t be changed once they are submitted.)
- **406.1.c** If a game ends due to a game loss penalty after players have selected and revealed their battlefields, players must use different battlefields for any following games in the match.
- **411.3.a** A deck check can be performed after the mulligan procedure is complete as long as play has not begun and each player’s hand is preserved.
- **416.5** Between games, players may refer to written notes they made prior to the match. They do not have to reveal these notes to opponents. Players may consult such notes during sideboarding and after battlefields are revealed, but must stop once opening hands are drawn (before mulligans).
- **502.4.b** If the end of match procedure has entered additional turns and where in the additional turns players are. Players must announce and acknowledge additional turns as they occur. (See 408 for more information on End of Match Procedure.)
- **502.11** Players are not required to share private information, and may bluff in regards to their private information.
- **505.2** Each iteration of a loop must contain identical, non-conditional actions performed on the same set of cards. To establish a loop, the looping player must be able to say with certainty exactly what each step of the loop will be, not just the outcome.
- **505.2.a** Loops must be fully deterministic; they may not rely on probability, decision trees, or conditional actions. Example:
- **505.2.a.1** Legal Loop: With enough Energy available, playing Grim Apothecary to a battlefield then using its trigger to return itself to hand 100 times.
- **505.2.a.2** Illegal Loop: A card reads "Recycle 3 cards from your trash: Ready a unit." A player with enough Energy available wants to use this to recycle 2 copies of Stupefy from their trash and 1 copy of Downstage Dramatics with an additional copy of Dramatics in hand, planning to play the Downstage Dramatics from hand, and then play each card as they draw it until there are 3 cards in the trash for them to recycle.
- **505.2.a.2.a** This is a non-deterministic loop because the order you play the cards is not guaranteed. If the loop only included copies of the same exact card draw spell, it would be deterministic.
- **505.2.b** Players can manually execute non-deterministic loops, but must do so in a timely fashion. Failure to do so is Slow Play. See 703.2 for more information on Slow Play.
- **505.13** Players cannot use loops to stall. When other actions are available, attempting to perform a loop that doesn’t change the game state is cheating. See 704.8 for more information about cheating.
- **506.3.e.10** A trigger would require a player to choose something, such as a unit or gear, even if there is only one legal choice. (Example: Abandoned Hall)
- **506.3.e.11** A trigger would cause a player to gain resources, such as XP or floating Power/Energy. (Example: Jhin, Murderous Artist)
- **506.3.e.12** A trigger would cause a card to change zones, such as a unit taking lethal damage. (Non-lethal damage is not an observable impact.)
- **508.8** Tokens must be represented by card objects that are able to represent ready state. They must be understandable and discernible by all players in the game where they are being used.
- **509.5** Players can always ready runes they just exhausted for Energy or unrecycle runes they just recycled for Power as long as no new action has been taken and focus has not been passed.
- **509.5.a** When changing decisions as described in 509.3. - 509.4., players can ready/unrecycle runes used for those decisions.
- **509.5.a.1** Example: A player exhausts two runes and plays Pouty Poro from hand to their base, then immediately decides they do not want to play it. They may return it to their hand and ready those two runes.
- **509.6** At high OPL, flexibility on gameplay decisions is not intended as a tool to allow players to reverse decisions after an opponent is required to indicate information that reveals that decision to be a tactical error.
- **509.6.a** Example: Player A moves their 3 Might unit to a battlefield and passes focus. Player B indicates their 2 Might unit at that battlefield is now at 4 Might due to their legend, Wuju Bladesman. Player A can no longer change their decision to move to that battlefield and pass focus.
- **509.6.b** Decisions made based on inaccurately represented public information may still be rewound at the discretion of the head judge. Inaccurately representing information to try to get a rewind is cheating.
- **601.3.c.6** Radiance (RAD)
- **601.3.c.6.a** Release Date: October 23rd, 2026
- **602.1.a.3** Players may utilize as many “blank” battlefields as they need for limited gameplay. See 602.3.d. below for more information on “blank” battlefields.
- **602.3.g** The sideboard process described in 406.1.h. does not apply to “best of 1” limited competitions.
- **701.4.b.1** If no randomized portion of the deck exists and it is possible, judges may complete remedies without shuffling. 701.4.b2. If judges can’t confidently determine the full randomized portion of a deck, they may shuffle as much of the deck as needed to perform a remedy.
- **701.5.b.3** When performing a simple rewind, if the action being rewound required the player to recycle/exhaust runes for payment, instruct that player to unrecycle/ready those runes.
- **702.1.b** Except for 702.17, repeated game play error [Warnings] can upgrade to [Game Loss].
- **702.1.d** Certain penalties, such as Forgetting to Draw Cards, that would usually be disadvantageous to the erroring player can upgrade in severity if they occur in a situation where the error is obviously advantageous for them.
- **702.1.d.1** Judges should only upgrade these penalties when the advantage is obvious. If it is ambiguous, judges should not upgrade.
- **702.1.e** Certain penalties, such as Channeling Too Few Runes, refer to a “full round cycle”. A full round cycle completes when the player whose turn the error occurred on begins their next turn.
- **702.8.b.1** If possible, perform a simple rewind to before the card or ability was put on the chain. If a simple rewind is not possible, instruct the players to continue playing.
- **702.13.b** Example: Moving a hidden card from a battlefield to a hand or Main Deck illegally.
- **702.13.c** Remedies for illegally added cards:
- **702.13.d** Remedies for illegally removed cards:
- **702.13.d.1** If the error is caught during the turn it occurred, and the card is easily identifiable and recoverable, return the card to the zone it came from and resume play.
- **702.13.d.2.a** If the error is caught on a later turn or the card is not easily identifiable and recoverable, this penalty upgrades to[Game Loss].
- **703.4.b** Remedies when a player forgets to select their Chosen Champion during setup: If the error is caught before the start of the first player’s turn, restore the deck to its correct state and instruct players to proceed.
- **703.4.b.1** At Casual OPL, if the error is caught after the start of the first player’s turn, instruct the erroring player to search the randomized portion of their main deck, their hand and sideboard for a legal chosen champion and place it in their chosen champion zone, then shuffle the randomized portion of their main deck. If the champion came from their hand, instruct them to draw a card.
- **703.4.b.1.a** If none are found, instruct players to continue playing with the erroring player’s chosen champion zone empty.
- **703.4.b.2** At Competitive OPL, if the error is caught after the start of the first player’s turn, a judge searches the randomized portion of the erroring player’s main deck and hand for a copy of their registered game one chosen champion and move it to their chosen champion zone, then shuffle the randomized portion of their main deck. If the champion came from their hand, instruct them to draw a card.
- **703.4.b.2.a** If none are found, instruct players to proceed with the erroring player’s chosen champion zone empty. At Professional OPL, if the error is caught after the start of the first player’s turn, instruct players to proceed with the erroring player’s chosen champion zone empty.
- **703.4.b.3** Purposely leaving your Chosen Champion zone empty during setup to gain an advantage is cheating. See 704.8 for more information about cheating.
- **703.4.c** Other Remedies:
- **703.4.c.5** At Professional OPL, extra cards in a player’s deck box as described in
- **703.4.d** For all deck presentation errors, if the cards required to correct the deck cannot be acquired in a timely manner, the head judge can issue a proxy.
- **703.9.g** Violating electronic devices policies will result in an outside assistance [Warning].
- **703.10.d** A Professional OPL, the penalty is upgraded to [Disqualification]. This should be clearly communicated to all players at the start of a Professional OPL event.
- **703.10.e** Knowingly attempting to improperly determine a winner or attempting to goad an opponent into doing so is cheating. See 704.8 for more information about cheating.
